L1279: **Abbreviations:** QW, once weekly.
L1280: a Stable doses of metformin (\ge 1500 mg/day) for \ge 3 months
L1281: b All tirzepatide doses were double-blinded.
L1282: **Trial 18F-MC-GPGH (SURPASS-3):** A multicenter, randomized, open-label, parallel-group phase 3 trial in subjects with T2D, inadequately controlled on stable doses of metformin (\ge 1500 mg/day) with or without a SGLT2i during the three months preceding to the start of the trial. This trial consisted of 3 periods: a 3-week screening/lead-in period, followed by a 52-week treatment period, and a 4-week safety follow-up period. Subjects were randomized in a 1:1:1:1 ratio (tirzepatide 5, 10, or 15 mg SC QW, or insulin degludec SC QD).
L1283: **Figure 18: Study Design of Trial GPGH**
L1284: **Source:** Reproduced from the Applicant's Clinical Study Report, Protocol 18F-MC-GPGH, labeled as Figure, page 28 of 94, available at: http://CDSESUB1\evsprod\nda215866\0001.m5\53-clin-stud-rep\535-rep-effc-safety-stud\type-2-diabetes-mellitus\5351-stud-rep-contr\i8f-mc-gpgh\gpgh-05-protocol--c-.pdf
L1285: **Abbreviations:** QD, daily; and QW, once weekly; and SGLT-2i, sodium-glucose cotransporter-2 inhibitor.

L1287: b The starting dose of insulin degludec was 10 IU/day ideally at bedtime, titrated to a fasting blood glucose (FBG) &lt;90 mg/dL, following a treat-to-target (TTT) algorithm.
L1288: **Trial 18F-MC-GPGM (SURPASS-4):** A multicenter, randomized, open-label, parallel-group, active-controlled, phase 3 trial in subjects with T2D with increased CV risk, inadequately controlled on stable doses of at least one and no more than three oral antihyperglycemic medications during the three months preceding to the start of the trial. This trial consisted of 3 periods: a 3-week screening/lead-in period, followed by a \ge 52 to 104-week treatment period (i.e., treatment continued for \ge 52 weeks from the time the last subject was randomized), and a 4-week safety follow-up period. Subjects were randomized in a 1:1:1:3 ratio (tirzepatide 5, 10, or 15 mg SC QW, or insulin glargine SC QD). The starting dose of insulin glargine was 10 units/day at bedtime, titrated to a FBG &lt;100 mg/dL, following a TTT algorithm.

From Week 8 to Week 16, subjects will continue the titration by a phone consultation or clinic visit every other week, with 3 weeks between Visits 13 and 14.
L1296: **Trial 18F-MC-GPGI (SURPASS-5):** A multicenter, randomized, double-blind, parallel-group, placebo-controlled, phase 3 study with 3 study periods in subjects with T2D, inadequately controlled on stable doses of titrated basal insulin glargine (&gt;0.25 units/kg/day or &gt;20 units/day) with or without metformin during the three months preceding to the start of the trial. This trial consisted of 3 periods: a 3-week screening/lead-in period, followed by a 40-week treatment period, and a 4-week safety follow-up period. Subjects were randomized in a 1:1:1:1 ratio (tirzepatide 5, 10, or 15 mg, or injectable placebo SC QW).

Maintenance period is from Weeks 24 to 40 (end of treatment/end of study), the period when insulin glargine dose is expected to be stable.
L1301: ## 13.4. Graphical Multiple-Testing Procedures for Type I Error Control
L1302: The graphical multiple-testing procedures for the primary and key secondary objectives for the treatment-regimen estimand and the efficacy estimand are presented for the five global phase 3 clinical trials (GPGK, GPGL, GPGH, GPGM, and GPGI). These procedures controlled the family-wise type 1 error rate for individual estimands.
L1303: **Trial 18F-MC-GPGK (SURPASS-1):** The graphical testing scheme325,326 used to control for type 1 error in trial GPGK is displayed in Figure 21. The primary and key secondary objective hypotheses were as follows:
L1304: - H5,1, H10,1, and H15,1: Superiority test of tirzepatide 5 mg, 10 mg, 15 mg versus placebo in HbA1c change from baseline at 40 weeks, respectively (each initially tested at a 0.01667 significance level).
- H5,2, H10,2, and H15,2: Superiority test of tirzepatide 5 mg, 10 mg, 15 mg versus placebo in body weight change from baseline at 40 weeks respectively.
- H5,3, H10,3, and H15,3: Superiority test of tirzepatide 5 mg, 10 mg, 15 mg versus placebo in proportion of subjects achieve HbA1c &lt;7% at 40 weeks respectively.
- H5,4, H10,4, and H15,4: Superiority test of tirzepatide 5 mg, 10 mg, 15 mg versus placebo in fasting serum glucose (FSG) change from baseline at 40 weeks respectively.
- H5,5, H10,5, and H15,5: Superiority test of tirzepatide 5 mg, 10 mg, 15 mg versus placebo in proportion of subjects achieve HbA1c &lt;5.7% at 40 weeks respectively.
L1305: **Figure 21: Type 1 Error Control Strategy (Primary &amp; Key Secondary Endpoints)—Trial GPGK**
L1306: **Source:** Reproduced/Adapted from the Applicant's 18F-MC-GPGK Statistical Analysis Plan (Version 3), pages 19-20 of 80, available at: C:\CDSESUB1\evsprod\NDA215866\0001\m5\53-clin-stud-rep\535-rep-effic-safety-stud\type-2-diabetes-mellitus\5351-stud-rep-contr\i8f-mc-gpgk\gpgk-13-statistical-analysis-plan-v3-including-v2.pdf
L1307: **Trial 18F-MC-GPGL (SURPASS-2):** The graphical testing scheme325 used to control for type 1 error in trial GPGL is displayed in Figure 22. The primary and key secondary objective hypotheses were as follows:
L1308: - H1, and H2: Noninferiority test of tirzepatide 10 mg and 15 mg versus semaglutide in HbA1c change from baseline at 40 weeks (each initially tested at a 0.025 significance level).
- H3, and H4: Superiority test of tirzepatide 10 mg, and 15 mg versus semaglutide in HbA1c change from baseline at 40 weeks.
- H5, and H6: Superiority test of tirzepatide 10 mg, and 15 mg versus semaglutide in body weight change from baseline at 40 weeks.
- H7: Noninferiority test of tirzepatide 5 mg versus semaglutide in HbA1c change from baseline at 40 weeks.
- H8: Superiority test of tirzepatide 5 mg versus semaglutide in HbA1c change from baseline at 40 weeks.
- H9, H10, and H11: Superiority test of tirzepatide 10 mg, 15 mg, and 5 mg versus semaglutide in proportion of subjects achieving HbA1c &lt;7% at 40 weeks.
- H12: Superiority test of tirzepatide 5 mg versus semaglutide in body weight change from baseline at 40 weeks.
- H13, and H14: Superiority test of tirzepatide 10 mg, and 15 mg versus semaglutide in proportion of subjects achieving HbA1c &lt; 5.7% at 40 weeks.
L1309: **Figure 22: Type 1 Error Control Strategy (Primary &amp; Key Secondary Endpoints)—Trial GPGL**
L1310: **Source:** Reproduced/Adapted from the Applicant's 18F-MC-GPGL Statistical Analysis Plan (Version 2), pages 21-22 of 44, available at: https://CDSESUB1\evsprod\NDA215866\0001\m5\53-clin-stud-rep\535-rep-effic-safety-stud\type-2-diabetes-mellitus\5351-stud-rep-contr\i8f-mc-gpgl\gpgl-stat-analysis-plan-v2.pdf
L1311: **Abbreviations:** A1c, hemoglobin A1c; NI, noninferiority; Sup, superiority; and wt, body weight.
L1312: **Trial 18F-MC-GPGH (SURPASS-3):** The graphical testing scheme used to control for type 1 error in trial GPGH is displayed in Figure 23. The primary and key secondary objective hypotheses were as follows:
L1313: - H15,1, H15,2, and H15,3 were evaluated hierarchically each at a 2-sided 0.025 significance level conditioned on successfully achieving the preceding objective. In parallel,
- H10,1, H10,2, and H10,3 were evaluated hierarchically each at a 2-sided 0.025 significance level conditioned on successfully achieving the preceding objective.
- If all objectives in #1 and #2 above were successfully established, H5,1, H5,2, and H5,3 were evaluated hierarchically, each at a 2-sided 0.05 significance level.
- If all objectives in only #1 or only #2 above were successfully established, H5,1, H5,2, and H5,3 were evaluated hierarchically, each at a 2-sided 0.025 significance level.
- If all objectives: H5,1, H5,2, and H5,3 were successfully established andIf all objectives in #1 and #2 above were successfully established, then H10,4, H15,4, and H5,4 were evaluated hierarchically each at a 2-sided 0.05 significance level conditioned on successfully achieving the preceding objective.
- If all objectives in only #1 or only #2 above were successfully established, then H10,4, H15,4, and H5,4 were evaluated hierarchically each at a 2-sided 0.025 significance level conditioned on successfully achieving the preceding objective.
- If all objectives in #3 and #4 above were successfully established, and at least 1 objective from #1 or #2 above was not successfully established, 100% of the unused alpha was recycled back to #1 or #2 above.
L1314: Figure 23: Type 1 Error Control Strategy (Primary &amp; Key Secondary Endpoints)—Trial GPGH
L1315: **Source:** Reproduced/Adapted from the Applicant's 18F-MC-GPGH Statistical Analysis Plan (Version 2), pages 20-22 of 43, available at: C:\CDS\SUB1\evsprod\NDA215866\0001\m5\53-clin-stud-rep\535-rep-effic-safety-stud\type-2-diabetes-mellitus\5351-stud-rep-contr\i8f-mc-gpgh\gpgh-stat-analysis-plan-v2.pdf
L1316: **Abbreviations:** HbA1c, hemoglobin A1c; LY, LY3298176 (tirzepatide); and vs, versus.
L1317: **Trial 18F-MC-GPGM (SURPASS-4):** The graphical testing scheme used to control for type 1 error in trial GPGM is displayed in Figure 24Figure 22. The primary and key secondary objective hypotheses were as follows:
L1318: - H15,1, H15,2, and H15,3 were evaluated hierarchically each at two-sided 0.025 significance level conditioned on the successfully achieving the preceding objective. In parallel,
- H10,1, H10,2, and H10,3 were evaluated hierarchically each at two-sided 0.025 significance level conditioned on the successfully achieving the preceding objective.
- If all objectives in #1 and #2 above were successfully established, H5,1, H5,2, and H5,3 were evaluated hierarchically, each at two-sided 0.05 significance level.
- If all objectives in only #1 or only #2 above were successfully established, H5,1, H5,2, and H5,3 were evaluated hierarchically, each at two-sided 0.025 significance level.
- If all objectives: H5,1, H5,2, and H5,3 were successfully established andif all objectives in #1 and #2 above were successfully established, then H10,4, H15,4 and H5,4 were evaluated hierarchically each at two-sided 0.05 significance level conditioned on the successfully achieving the preceding objective.
- if all objectives in only #1 or only #2 above were successfully established, then H10,4, H15,4 and H5,4 were evaluated hierarchically each at two-sided 0.025 significance level conditioned on the successfully achieving the preceding objective.
- If all objectives in #3 and #4 above were successfully established, and at least 1 objective from #1 or #2 above was not successfully established, 100% of the unused alpha recycled back to #1 or #2 above.

L1322: **Trial 18F-MC-GPGI (SURPASS-5):** The graphical testing scheme used to control for type 1 error in trial GPGI is displayed in Figure 25. The primary and key secondary objective hypotheses were as follows:
L1323: - H_{15,1} and H_{15,2} were evaluated hierarchically each at a 2-sided 0.025 significance level conditioned on successfully achieving the preceding objective. In parallel,
- H_{10,1} and H_{10,2} were evaluated hierarchically each at a 2-sided 0.025 significance level conditioned on successfully achieving the preceding objective.
- If all objectives in #1 and #2 above were successfully established, H_{5,1} and H_{5,2} were evaluated hierarchically, each at a 2-sided 0.05 significance level.
- If all objectives in only #1 or only #2 above were successfully established, H_{5,1} and H_{5,2} were evaluated hierarchically, each at a 2-sided 0.025 significance level.
- If both objectives: H_{5,1} and H_{5,2} were successfully established andIf all objectives in #1 and #2 above were successfully established, then H_{10,3}, H_{15,3}, H_{10,4}, H_{15,4}, H_{5,3}, H_{5,4}, H_{15,5}, and H_{10,5} were evaluated hierarchically each at a 2-sided 0.05 significance level conditioned on successfully achieving the preceding objective.
- If all objectives in only #1 or only #2 above were successfully established, then H_{10,3}, H_{15,3}, H_{10,4}, H_{15,4}, H_{5,3}, H_{5,4}, H_{15,5}, and H_{10,5} were evaluated hierarchically each at a 2-sided 0.025 significance level conditioned on successfully achieving the preceding objective.
- If all objectives in #3, #4, and #5 above were successfully established, and at least 1 objective from #1 or #2 above was not successfully established, 100% of the unused alpha was recycled back to #1 or #2 above.

L1427: Line graph showing Mean HbA1c Over Time (Baseline to Week 104) for Trial GPGM (mITT- Efficacy). The X-axis represents Weeks (BL, 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 42, 52, 78, 104). The left Y-axis shows LSMean + SE for HbA1c (%) (ranging from 5.0 to 9.5). The right Y-axis shows LSMean + SE for HbA1c (mmol/mol) (ranging from 31.2 to 80.3). Four lines are plotted: TZP 5mg (light blue), TZP 10mg (dark blue), TZP 15mg (purple), and Insulin Glargine (dashed gray). All tirzepatide doses show a rapid initial decrease in HbA1c from baseline (BL, approximately 8.5%) and stabilize significantly lower than the Insulin Glargine group (which stabilizes around 7.5%) by Week 104. The 10mg and 15mg doses appear slightly lower than the 5mg dose after Week 24.
L1428: Figure 6 is a scatter plot titled "Percent of Subjects Remaining on Study Medication vs. Study Day" for the Actual Treatment for Period 01. The Y-axis represents the Percent of Subjects Remaining on Study Medication (0% to 100%), and the X-axis represents the Study Day (0 to 700). Data points are shown for three tirzepatide treatment arms: TZP 15mg (blue dots), TZP 10mg (yellow/green dots), and TZP 5mg (red dots).
The plot illustrates the percentage of subjects remaining on study medication over time for each dose group. All three arms show a high initial retention rate (around 90-100% at Day 0). The retention rate generally decreases over time, with the TZP 5mg arm showing the steepest decline after approximately Day 300, dropping below 20% by Day 700. The TZP 10mg and TZP 15mg arms maintain higher retention rates, generally staying above 40% throughout the study period.
Scatter plot showing Percent of Subjects Remaining on Study Medication vs. Study Day for TZP 5mg, TZP 10mg, and TZP 15mg treatment arms.
L1429: Figure 7: Mean (SE) Change from Baseline in Pulse Rate Over Time (AS1). The figure consists of two plots. The top plot is a line graph showing Pulse Rate (bpm) on the Y-axis (ranging from 70 to 80) versus Analysis Visit (Baseline, Week 4, Week 8, Week 12, Week 16, Week 20, Week 24, Week 32, Week 40, Safety Follow-up) on the X-axis. Four treatment arms are compared: TZP 15mg (green), TZP 10mg (yellow), TZP 5mg (blue), and Placebo (gray). Pulse rates generally increase from Baseline to Week 16/24, then decrease towards Safety Follow-up. The TZP 15mg arm shows the highest pulse rate, peaking around 79 bpm at Week 16/24, while the Placebo arm shows the lowest, peaking around 75 bpm. Error bars represent standard error. The bottom plot is a bar chart showing the Count of subjects (Y-axis, ranging from 0 to 250) across the same Analysis Visits for the four treatment arms. The counts are relatively stable across visits, ranging from approximately 220 to 240 subjects per arm.

L1431: Figure 9: Mean (SE) Plus Rate Changes from Baseline Over Time – Japanese Subgroup (AS2). The figure displays two plots. The top plot, 'Average Measurements Across Analysis Visit', shows Pulse Rate (bpm) on the Y-axis (70 to 82) versus Analysis Visit (Baseline, Week 4, Week 8, Week 12, Week 16, Week 20, Week 24, Week 32, Week 40, Week 52, Safety Follow-up) on the X-axis. Four tirzepatide dose arms (TZP 15mg, TZP 10mg, TZP 5mg) and a placebo group are compared. Pulse rates generally increase from baseline, peak around Week 20/24, and then decrease. The 15mg arm shows the highest peak pulse rate (around 82 bpm) and the highest rate of increase from baseline (around 13.7%). The bottom plot shows the count of subjects meeting abnormal pulse rate criteria (Y-axis, 0 to 350) across the same analysis visits. The counts for the 15mg arm are significantly higher than the other arms, especially after Week 12, indicating a dose-related increase in abnormal pulse rate criteria.
L1432: Figure 10 displays two panels of data related to tirzepatide pharmacokinetics and heart rate response.
The upper panel plots Mean Predicted \DeltaHR (Heart Rate increase in bpm) versus Observed Tirzepatide Concentrations (ng/mL). The x-axis ranges from 0 to 6000 ng/mL. The y-axis ranges from 0 to 20 bpm. Data is stratified by ethnicity (Japanese: Yes/Blue, No/Red).
The solid blue line represents the model-predicted mean \DeltaHR for Japanese subjects, showing a steeper, non-linear increase in \DeltaHR with concentration, reaching approximately 15 bpm at 5000 ng/mL.
The solid red line represents the model-predicted mean \DeltaHR for non-Japanese subjects, showing a flatter, non-linear increase, reaching approximately 8 bpm at 5000 ng/mL.
Black circles and triangles represent the mean of the observed \DeltaHR in non-Japanese and Japanese subjects, respectively, with vertical error bars indicating the 90% Confidence Interval (CI) around the mean.
Shared shaded areas around the model-predicted mean \DeltaHR lines represent the 90% CI around the mean \DeltaHR.
The lower panel plots Dose (mg) versus Predicted Steady-state C_{max} (ng/mL). The x-axis ranges from 0 to 6000 ng/mL. The y-axis ranges from 5 to 15 mg. Box plots show the distribution of the expected tirzepatide steady-state C_{max} (C_{max,ss}) predicted using individual PK parameters of phase 3 studies patients and derived from the Applicant's PK model, for doses of 5 mg, 10 mg, and 15 mg.
Red box plots represent non-Japanese subjects.
Blue box plots represent Japanese subjects.
The predicted C_{max,ss} increases non-linearly with dose for both groups.
Japanese subjects consistently show higher predicted C_{max,ss} values than non-Japanese subjects across all doses.

L1437: Figure 15 is a Kaplan-Meier plot showing the Time to First Occurrence of the Composite MACE-4 Endpoint (AS5) for two groups: TZP_ALL (Tirzepatide) and Pooled Comparator. The X-axis represents Time from First Dose (Weeks), ranging from 0 to 120. The Y-axis represents Patients with an Event (%), ranging from 0 to 10.

L1444: Figure 22 is a graphical testing scheme (Bonferroni-adjusted alpha spending function) used to control for type 1 error across 14 hypotheses (H1 through H14) in Trial GPGL. The hypotheses are grouped into four main categories based on endpoint and dose comparison:
Noninferiority (NI) HbA1c Change: H1 (NI A1c, 15 mg), H2 (NI A1c, 10 mg), and H7 (NI A1c, 5 mg). H1 and H2 are initially tested at \alpha=0.025.
Superiority (Sup) HbA1c Change: H3 (Sup A1c, 15 mg), H4 (Sup A1c, 10 mg), H5 (Sup wt, 15 mg), H6 (Sup wt, 10 mg), H8 (Sup A1c, 5 mg), H9 (Sup A1c<7%, 15 mg), H10 (Sup A1c<7%, 10 mg), H11 (Sup A1c<7%, 5 mg), H13 (Sup A1c<5.7%, 15 mg), and H14 (Sup A1c<5.7%, 10 mg).
Superiority (Sup) Body Weight Change: H5, H6, H12 (Sup wt, 5 mg).
Superiority (Sup) Proportion Achieving HbA1c <7%: H9, H10, H11.
Superiority (Sup) Proportion Achieving HbA1c <5.7%: H13, H14.
The figure displays the alpha spending function (alpha values) for each hypothesis and the connections between them, illustrating the sequential testing strategy. Key alpha values shown include 0.025, 0.5, 0.2, 0.8, 0.335, 0.066, 0.25, 0.1, and 1.0.
